# `pcloud-backends`

**Maturity:** Internal stable

**Version:** `0.8.1-beta`

**Directory:** `crates/pcloud-backends`

**Manifest:** [`crates/pcloud-backends/Cargo.toml`](https://github.com/ezechiel203/pcloud-rs/blob/main/crates/pcloud-backends/Cargo.toml)

Backend implementations split out of pcloud-daemon (P6.1).

## Feature-family profile

**Why it exists.** Turn low-level pCloud protocol methods into coherent filesystem, account, sharing, backup, transfer, residency, and snapshot behaviors.

**What it is good for.** Daemon business logic and the canonical RemoteFs service used by CLI, SDK, sync, mount, and web adapters.

**Why it is good at that job.** ID-first operations, narrow subsystem backends, durable transfer state, and a single remote namespace prevent competing interpretations of cloud state.

## Direct dependencies

`base64`, `filetime`, `getrandom`, `libc`, `log`, `pcloud-auth`, `pcloud-cache`, `pcloud-config`, `pcloud-crypto`, `pcloud-engine`, `pcloud-fs`, `pcloud-ipc`, `pcloud-model`, `pcloud-observability`, `pcloud-p2p`, `pcloud-proto`, `pcloud-resilience`, `pcloud-secret`, `pcloud-store`, `rsa`, `rusqlite`, `serde`, `serde_json`, `sha1`, `sha2`, `sha3`, `tar`, `tempfile`, `thiserror`, `zeroize`, `zstd`

## Cargo features

No declared package features.

## Usage guidance

Core workspace code may depend on this contract. External applications should prefer `pcloud-sdk` unless they intentionally own the lower-level runtime.
